\end{enumerate}

\section*{Goal}\label{goal-9}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Goal}

\markright{Goal}

Produce draft versions of every A7 file from the Quarto starter.
Tomorrow's polish will make them assignment-ready; today's work makes
sure the bones are there.

\section*{The 60-minute studio
sequence}\label{the-60-minute-studio-sequence}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{The 60-minute studio sequence}

\markright{The 60-minute studio sequence}

You will move through six short steps. Each step produces one or two
artifacts.

\subsection*{Step 1 --- Audience and question (10
min)}\label{step-1-audience-and-question-10-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 1 --- Audience and question (10
min)}

Write two short paragraphs in \texttt{dashboard-adaptation-note.md}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Audience.} One specific role and what they need to decide.
\item
  \textbf{Question.} Phrased the way the audience would phrase it.
\end{itemize}

Use the templates from
\hyperref[from-one-finding-to-a-prototype-the-1-1-1-1-1-rule]{dash02}.
Be specific.

\subsection*{Step 2 --- Identify your control and visualization (5
min)}\label{step-2-identify-your-control-and-visualization-5-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 2 --- Identify your control and
visualization (5 min)}

In the same note, add:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Control.} One control and the values it can take.
\item
  \textbf{Visualization.} One chart and what it shows when the control
  changes.
\end{itemize}

For most students, this is the existing \texttt{selected\_income}
control and the line chart of mean BMI by cycle from the starter. That
is fine.

\subsection*{Step 3 --- Pick one audience-facing change (10
min)}\label{step-3-pick-one-audience-facing-change-10-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 3 --- Pick one audience-facing
change (10 min)}

Read your starter file with the eyes of your stated audience. Find one
element that could be clearer --- a label, a caption, a help text line,
a README sentence, or a limitation. Pick the one that would help the
audience most.

Write \texttt{before-after-text.md}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The original text.
\item
  The revised text.
\item
  One sentence on why the change helps.
\item
  One sentence confirming no analytic logic changed (see
  \hyperref[audience-facing-text-what-you-can-and-cannot-change-for-a7]{dash04}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Step 4 --- Apply the change and test (15
min)}\label{step-4-apply-the-change-and-test-15-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 4 --- Apply the change and test
(15 min)}

Edit your local copy of the starter file with the revised text. Then run
the six-step test routine from
\hyperref[testing-privacy-safe-publishing-and-the-shiny-demo]{dash05}:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Render.
\item
  Change the control to a different value.
\item
  Render again.
\item
  Verify the caption.
\item
  Open the HTML preview.
\item
  Record the test in \texttt{testing-log.md}.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{tcolorbox}[enhanced jigsaw, colback=white, opacitybacktitle=0.6, breakable, coltitle=black, bottomtitle=1mm, bottomrule=.15mm, left=2mm, opacityback=0, leftrule=.75mm, rightrule=.15mm, toptitle=1mm, colbacktitle=quarto-callout-note-color!10!white, titlerule=0mm, colframe=quarto-callout-note-color-frame, arc=.35mm, title=\textcolor{quarto-callout-note-color}{\faInfo}\hspace{0.5em}{Tester note}, toprule=.15mm]

Capture: a screenshot of the rendered ``after'' state showing the
revised audience-facing element clearly visible. Beginners need to see
what a successful adaptation looks like before they attempt their own.

\end{tcolorbox}

\subsection*{Step 5 --- Write the limitation and privacy check (10
min)}\label{step-5-write-the-limitation-and-privacy-check-10-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 5 --- Write the limitation and
privacy check (10 min)}

In \texttt{limitation-note.md}, write \textbf{one sentence} stating what
the dashboard does \emph{not} support. Use the pattern from
\hyperref[four-audit-categories-for-ai-drafted-eda]{eda05} on
descriptive-vs-causal.

In \texttt{privacy-check.md}, write the \textbf{three sentences} from
\hyperref[testing-privacy-safe-publishing-and-the-shiny-demo]{dash05}:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  One naming the data source and classroom-use boundary.
\item
  One confirming aggregate-only outputs.
\item
  One confirming no row-level data was shared with external AI.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{Step 6 --- Reproducibility and AI use (10
min)}\label{step-6-reproducibility-and-ai-use-10-min}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{Step 6 --- Reproducibility and AI use
(10 min)}

In \texttt{reproducibility-note.md}, write one paragraph naming:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The command or render path you used.
\item
  The relative path to the dataset.
\item
  A sentence on whether the dashboard renders in a fresh Codespace.
\end{itemize}

In \texttt{ai-use-note.md}, name what AI helped with (if anything) and
how you verified the result.

\section*{The eight A7 deliverables,
explained}\label{the-eight-a7-deliverables-explained}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{The eight A7 deliverables, explained}

\markright{The eight A7 deliverables, explained}

Each file goes in
\texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/submission/}.

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{1.
\texttt{dashboard-adaptation-note.md}}{1. dashboard-adaptation-note.md}}\label{dashboard-adaptation-note.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{1.
\texttt{dashboard-adaptation-note.md}}

Audience, question, control, visualization, the element you changed, and
the KT rationale. Pattern after the studio Step 1 and Step 2 outputs.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} a peer can read this and identify your audience and
  question without seeing your code.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{2.
\texttt{before-after-text.md}}{2. before-after-text.md}}\label{before-after-text.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2. \texttt{before-after-text.md}}

The original text, the revised text, why the change helps the audience,
and a confirmation that no analytic logic changed. Pattern after the
studio Step 3 output.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} the change is genuinely audience-facing text. If you
  cannot say ``no analytic logic changed'', the change does not belong
  in A7 (see
  \hyperref[audience-facing-text-what-you-can-and-cannot-change-for-a7]{dash04}).
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{3.
\texttt{testing-log.md}}{3. testing-log.md}}\label{testing-log.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{3. \texttt{testing-log.md}}

Date tested, the command or render path you used, the values you tried,
and the result. Pattern after the studio Step 4 output.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} another student could reproduce your test by
  following your log.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{4.
\texttt{limitation-note.md}}{4. limitation-note.md}}\label{limitation-note.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{4. \texttt{limitation-note.md}}

One sentence on what the dashboard does not support. Use the
descriptive-vs-causal patterns from
\hyperref[four-audit-categories-for-ai-drafted-eda]{eda05}.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} the sentence belongs \emph{in or beside} the
  dashboard, not just in a separate file.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{5.
\texttt{privacy-check.md}}{5. privacy-check.md}}\label{privacy-check.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{5. \texttt{privacy-check.md}}

Three sentences from
\hyperref[testing-privacy-safe-publishing-and-the-shiny-demo]{dash05}:
data source and classroom-use boundary, aggregate-only confirmation, no
row-level data shared with AI.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} three sentences, each specific.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{6.
\texttt{dashboard-preview.png}}{6. dashboard-preview.png}}\label{dashboard-preview.png}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{6. \texttt{dashboard-preview.png}}

A screenshot or exported preview of the affected dashboard area (after
the text change).

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} the preview clearly shows the revised audience-facing
  element. No row-level table previews.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{7.
\texttt{reproducibility-note.md}}{7. reproducibility-note.md}}\label{reproducibility-note.md}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{7. \texttt{reproducibility-note.md}}

One paragraph naming the render command, the relative path, and whether
the dashboard renders in a fresh Codespace.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} the path is relative, and the note matches what
  \texttt{testing-log.md} says.
\end{itemize}

\subsection*{\texorpdfstring{8.
\texttt{ai-use-note.md}}{8. ai-use-note.md}}\label{ai-use-note.md-2}
\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{8. \texttt{ai-use-note.md}}

What AI helped with (or a sentence saying you did not use it) and how
you verified the result.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Verify:} specific (which prompt? which file?), not vague.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Reproducibility checklist for
A7}\label{reproducibility-checklist-for-a7}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Reproducibility checklist for A7}

\markright{Reproducibility checklist for A7}

Before you click submit:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The adapted Quarto file renders in a fresh Codespace.
\item
  The data path is relative
  (\texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}).
\item
  The preview screenshot shows the revised text change clearly.
\item
  The dashboard contains a visible limitation sentence (not just in a
  separate file).
\item
  No row-level data appears in any screenshot or shared output.
\item
  All eight files are committed and pushed.
\end{itemize}

\section*{A pathway decision template you can
reuse}\label{a-pathway-decision-template-you-can-reuse}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{A pathway decision template you can
reuse}

\markright{A pathway decision template you can reuse}

When the term-project conversation comes up later in the term, your team
will face the same pathway choice for the final portfolio. Use this
short template to document the decision:

\begin{quote}
Our audience is \_\_\_\_\_\_. We need \_\_\_\_\_\_ kind of
interactivity. The simplest pathway that serves this need is
\textbf{{[}Quarto dashboard-style page / Shiny app / static HTML/JS{]}}.
{[}If Shiny or static HTML/JS:{]} We have instructor approval recorded
on \_\_\_\_\_\_.
\end{quote}

Default to Quarto. Justify deviations.

\item
  Cached classroom data kept separate from any raw retrieval workflow.
\item
  Row-level data never uploaded to external AI tools.
\end{itemize}

\chapter*{Dynamic Presentations}\label{dynamic-presentations}

\section*{Overview}\label{overview-8}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Overview}

\markright{Overview}

Data analysis is useful only when the right audience can understand what
the evidence does and does not say. This week turns one dashboard
finding into a short, plain-language scientific communication product
using Quarto slides.

\section*{Connection}\label{connection-8}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Connection}

\markright{Connection}

Week 8 made the dashboard audience-facing. This week turns one dashboard
plot or table into a short slide story with a clear takeaway, evidence,
and limitation. Next week you will expand that communication into a
reproducible report.

\section*{Dashboard Case Study: NHANES
Equity}\label{dashboard-case-study-nhanes-equity-1}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Dashboard Case Study: NHANES Equity}

\markright{Dashboard Case Study: NHANES Equity}

The \textbf{NHANES Health Equity Dashboard} is now the foreground case
study. Use it as a small knowledge-translation product built on the
cached NHANES equity dataset.

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  App code: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/app/app.R}
\item
  Cached data:
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.rds} and
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Optional rebuild script:
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/scripts/build\_nhanes\_equity.R}
\end{itemize}

Run the dashboard from the repository root:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{shiny}\SpecialCharTok{::}\FunctionTok{runApp}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"examples/nhanes{-}equity/app"}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The app is offline-first: it loads the local RDS cache when available.
Rebuilding from CDC uses \texttt{nhanesA}, requires internet access, and
is optional unless assigned.

\item
  Week 10 report template:
  \texttt{weeks/week10-reporting-publishing/report-template.qmd}

\section*{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-2}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Learning Objectives}

\markright{Learning Objectives}

By the end of this week, you should be able to:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  identify the audience and decision context for a result
\item
  translate one technical finding into plain language
\item
  build a short Quarto revealjs slide deck or slide outline
\item
  cite the data source for a figure or table
\item
  use peer feedback to reduce overclaiming and improve clarity
\end{itemize}

\section*{In-Class Plan}\label{in-class-plan}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{In-Class Plan}

\markright{In-Class Plan}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Choose one plot or table from prior work.
\item
  Draft a three-slide story using \href{starter-slides.qmd}{Starter
  Slides}.
\item
  Rewrite one technical sentence in plain language.
\item
  Complete peer feedback using
  \href{revealjs-peer-feedback.qmd}{Revealjs and Peer Feedback}.
\item
  Revise the takeaway, caption, or limitation before submitting
  \href{../../assignments/assignment08-communication/README.md}{Assignment
  8}.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{Plain-Language Translation}\label{plain-language-translation}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Plain-Language Translation}

\markright{Plain-Language Translation}

Start with a technical sentence such as:

\begin{quote}
Mean BMI differs by income group in the prepared NHANES classroom
dataset.
\end{quote}

Translate it for a nontechnical audience:

\begin{quote}
In this classroom dataset, average BMI is not the same across income
groups, but this comparison is descriptive and does not prove income
causes BMI differences.
\end{quote}

Your translation should keep the evidence honest. Do not remove the
limitation just to make the message sound stronger.

\section*{Citation And Accessibility}\label{citation-and-accessibility}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Citation And Accessibility}

\markright{Citation And Accessibility}

Before submission, check that:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the data source is named on the slide or in speaker notes
\item
  any figure has a readable title, axis labels, and units
\item
  colors are not the only way to understand groups
\item
  text is large enough to read during a live presentation
\item
  the limitation is visible, not hidden in tiny text
\item
  AI-assisted wording has been checked against the actual output
\end{itemize}

\section*{Student Output}\label{student-output-6}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Student Output}

\markright{Student Output}

Submit a 3-5 slide deck or slide outline, a plain-language summary, peer
feedback notes, and an AI-use note. Assignment link:
\href{../../assignments/assignment08-communication/README.md}{Assignment
8}.

\section*{Definition Of Done}\label{definition-of-done-5}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Definition Of Done}

\markright{Definition Of Done}

Week 9 is done when the slide story renders or is ready to render, the
main message is understandable without code, and a peer can identify the
evidence and limitation.

\chapter*{Revealjs And Peer Feedback}\label{revealjs-and-peer-feedback}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Revealjs And Peer Feedback}

\markboth{Revealjs And Peer Feedback}{Revealjs And Peer Feedback}

\section*{Goal}\label{goal-10}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Goal}

\markright{Goal}

Turn one dashboard or report finding into a short scientific
communication product and practice structured peer feedback.

\section*{Three-Slide Structure}\label{three-slide-structure}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Three-Slide Structure}

\markright{Three-Slide Structure}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Question and audience:} Who needs this finding, and why?
\item
  \textbf{Evidence:} Show one plot or table and state the descriptive
  pattern.
\item
  \textbf{Limitation and action:} Name what the data can and cannot
  support, then state the next decision or question.
\end{enumerate}

Use \href{starter-slides.qmd}{Starter Slides} if you want a renderable
revealjs scaffold.

\section*{Plain-Language Check}\label{plain-language-check}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Plain-Language Check}

\markright{Plain-Language Check}

For each slide, ask:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  What is the one sentence the audience should remember?
\item
  Which word or phrase would confuse someone outside the course?
\item
  What limitation must remain visible to avoid overclaiming?
\end{itemize}

\section*{Peer Feedback Form}\label{peer-feedback-form}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Peer Feedback Form}

\markright{Peer Feedback Form}

Copy this table into your submission.

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}ll@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
Prompt & Peer note \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Main message in one sentence & \\
Intended audience & \\
Evidence shown & \\
Limitation or caution shown & \\
Wording that needs plain-language revision & \\
Accessibility issue, if any & \\
One concrete improvement before submission & \\
\end{longtable}

\section*{Citation And Accessibility
Checklist}\label{citation-and-accessibility-checklist}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Citation And Accessibility Checklist}

\markright{Citation And Accessibility Checklist}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Data source is named.
\item
  Figure or table has a clear title.
\item
  Axes, units, and group labels are readable.
\item
  Color choices work without relying on color alone.
\item
  Slide text is brief enough to read during a talk.
\item
  Limitation is visible.
\item
  AI-assisted wording has been checked against the evidence.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Student Output}\label{student-output-7}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Student Output}

\markright{Student Output}

Submit the rendered slide deck or slide outline, completed peer feedback
form, plain-language summary, and AI-use note required by
\href{../../assignments/assignment08-communication/README.md}{Assignment
8}.

\part{Week 10: Writing \& Publishing Reports}

\chapter*{Reproducible Reporting}\label{reproducible-reporting}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Reproducible Reporting}

\markboth{Reproducible Reporting}{Reproducible Reporting}

\section*{Overview}\label{overview-9}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Overview}

\markright{Overview}

This chapter simulates the final stretch of a research project: writing
the manuscript. Students learn to assemble their analysis, code, and
writing into a single ``dynamic report'' that automatically updates
numbers and figures if the data changes. You will explore advanced
publishing workflows to create professional, citation-ready portfolios
that demonstrate your ability to execute end-to-end data science.

\section*{Connection}\label{connection-9}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Connection}

\markright{Connection}

Week 9 turned one result into a short slide story. This week expands the
same evidence into a reproducible report with code-generated outputs,
citations, and limitations. Next week you will stress-test whether the
report and dashboard work in a fresh environment.

\section*{In-Class Activity}\label{in-class-activity-4}

\markright{In-Class Activity}

\begin{itemize}

  Draft a short Quarto report that complements the dashboard rather than

\section*{Publishing Check}\label{publishing-check}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Publishing Check}

\markright{Publishing Check}

For public GitHub Pages output, confirm that the report does not expose
restricted data, private URLs, tokens, or sensitive examples. For
private preview, submit the repository link on Canvas and grant course
staff access.

\section*{Grounding Audit}\label{grounding-audit}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Grounding Audit}

\markright{Grounding Audit}

Before submission, choose three claims in the report and trace each one
to a table, figure, code chunk, or citation. Revise any claim that
cannot be grounded.

\section*{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-6}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Definition of Done}

\markright{Definition of Done}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The report renders from source.
\item
  Citations resolve.
\item
  Outputs are regenerated by code rather than pasted manually.
\item
  The README explains how to reproduce the report.
\end{itemize}

\chapter*{Report Template: NHANES Equity
Summary}\label{report-template-nhanes-equity-summary}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Report Template: NHANES Equity Summary}

\markboth{Report Template: NHANES Equity Summary}{Report Template:
NHANES Equity Summary}

\section*{Standalone YAML}\label{standalone-yaml}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Standalone YAML}

\markright{Standalone YAML}

If you copy this template into a project report outside the book, start
the file with this YAML header:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\PreprocessorTok{{-}{-}{-}}
\FunctionTok{title}\KeywordTok{:}\AttributeTok{ }\StringTok{"NHANES Equity Summary"}
\FunctionTok{format}\KeywordTok{:}\AttributeTok{ html}
\FunctionTok{bibliography}\KeywordTok{:}\AttributeTok{ ../../references.bib}
\PreprocessorTok{{-}{-}{-}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section*{Purpose}\label{purpose-2}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Purpose}

\markright{Purpose}

This runnable template shows how to pair a dashboard with a short
reproducible report. The dashboard supports exploration; the report
fixes one question, documents the data, and cites the source.

\section*{Question}\label{question-1}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Question}

\markright{Question}

How does mean BMI vary by income group among adults age 20-80 in the
prepared NHANES Health Equity classroom dataset?

\section*{Data Source}\label{data-source}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Data Source}

\markright{Data Source}

The report uses the cached CSV snapshot at
\texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}. The source
data are derived from CDC/NCHS NHANES public-use files (Centers for
Disease Control and Prevention, National Center for Health Statistics
2026).

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(readr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(dplyr)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(ggplot2)}
\FunctionTok{library}\NormalTok{(knitr)}

\NormalTok{candidate\_paths }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{c}\NormalTok{(}
  \StringTok{"examples/nhanes{-}equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv"}\NormalTok{,}
  \StringTok{"../../examples/nhanes{-}equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv"}
\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{data\_path }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ candidate\_paths[}\FunctionTok{file.exists}\NormalTok{(candidate\_paths)][}\DecValTok{1}\NormalTok{]}
\ControlFlowTok{if}\NormalTok{ (}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(data\_path)) \{}
  \FunctionTok{stop}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"Could not find examples/nhanes{-}equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv"}\NormalTok{)}
\NormalTok{\}}

\NormalTok{nhanes }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}} \FunctionTok{read\_csv}\NormalTok{(data\_path, }\AttributeTok{show\_col\_types =} \ConstantTok{FALSE}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section*{Methods}\label{methods}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Methods}

\markright{Methods}

We restricted the classroom dataset to adults age 20-80 and summarized
BMI by income group. The summaries are unweighted and descriptive. They
should not be interpreted as causal effects or national population
estimates.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{analysis\_df }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ nhanes }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(Age }\SpecialCharTok{\textgreater{}=} \DecValTok{20}\NormalTok{, Age }\SpecialCharTok{\textless{}=} \DecValTok{80}\NormalTok{)}

\NormalTok{missingness }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ analysis\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}
    \AttributeTok{rows =} \FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{(),}
    \AttributeTok{missing\_bmi =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(BMI)),}
    \AttributeTok{missing\_income =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup)),}
    \AttributeTok{missing\_age =} \FunctionTok{sum}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(Age))}
\NormalTok{  )}

\FunctionTok{kable}\NormalTok{(missingness, }\AttributeTok{caption =} \StringTok{"Missingness check for key report variables."}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}rrrr@{}}
\caption{Missingness check for key report variables.}\tabularnewline
\toprule\noalign{}
rows & missing\_bmi & missing\_income & missing\_age \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endfirsthead
\toprule\noalign{}
rows & missing\_bmi & missing\_income & missing\_age \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
57137 & 1009 & 5395 & 0 \\
\end{longtable}

\section*{Results}\label{results}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Results}

\markright{Results}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{income\_summary }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ analysis\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{!}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(BMI), }\SpecialCharTok{!}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup)) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}
    \AttributeTok{n =} \FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{(),}
    \AttributeTok{mean\_bmi =} \FunctionTok{mean}\NormalTok{(BMI),}
    \AttributeTok{sd\_bmi =} \FunctionTok{sd}\NormalTok{(BMI),}
    \AttributeTok{.groups =} \StringTok{"drop"}
\NormalTok{  ) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{mutate}\NormalTok{(}
    \StringTok{\textasciigrave{}}\AttributeTok{BMI, mean (SD)}\StringTok{\textasciigrave{}} \OtherTok{=} \FunctionTok{sprintf}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"\%.1f (\%.1f)"}\NormalTok{, mean\_bmi, sd\_bmi)}
\NormalTok{  ) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{select}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup, n, }\StringTok{\textasciigrave{}}\AttributeTok{BMI, mean (SD)}\StringTok{\textasciigrave{}}\NormalTok{)}

\FunctionTok{kable}\NormalTok{(income\_summary, }\AttributeTok{caption =} \StringTok{"Descriptive BMI summary by income group."}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lrl@{}}
\caption{Descriptive BMI summary by income group.}\tabularnewline
\toprule\noalign{}
IncomeGroup & n & BMI, mean (SD) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endfirsthead
\toprule\noalign{}
IncomeGroup & n & BMI, mean (SD) \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
High Income (\textgreater3.5) & 16330 & 28.6 (6.3) \\
Low Income (\textless1.3) & 15293 & 29.4 (7.5) \\
Middle Income & 19228 & 29.3 (7.0) \\
\end{longtable}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{plot\_df }\OtherTok{\textless{}{-}}\NormalTok{ analysis\_df }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{filter}\NormalTok{(}\SpecialCharTok{!}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(BMI), }\SpecialCharTok{!}\FunctionTok{is.na}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup)) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{group\_by}\NormalTok{(IncomeGroup) }\SpecialCharTok{|\textgreater{}}
  \FunctionTok{summarise}\NormalTok{(}
    \AttributeTok{n =} \FunctionTok{n}\NormalTok{(),}
    \AttributeTok{mean\_bmi =} \FunctionTok{mean}\NormalTok{(BMI),}
    \AttributeTok{.groups =} \StringTok{"drop"}
\NormalTok{  )}

\FunctionTok{ggplot}\NormalTok{(plot\_df, }\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{x =}\NormalTok{ IncomeGroup, }\AttributeTok{y =}\NormalTok{ mean\_bmi)) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_col}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{fill =} \StringTok{"\#2C7FB8"}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{geom\_text}\NormalTok{(}\FunctionTok{aes}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{label =} \FunctionTok{paste0}\NormalTok{(}\StringTok{"n="}\NormalTok{, n)), }\AttributeTok{vjust =} \SpecialCharTok{{-}}\FloatTok{0.4}\NormalTok{, }\AttributeTok{size =} \FloatTok{3.5}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{labs}\NormalTok{(}
    \AttributeTok{title =} \StringTok{"Mean BMI by income group"}\NormalTok{,}
    \AttributeTok{subtitle =} \StringTok{"Adults age 20{-}80; unweighted descriptive summary"}\NormalTok{,}
    \AttributeTok{x =} \StringTok{"Income group"}\NormalTok{,}
    \AttributeTok{y =} \StringTok{"Mean BMI"}
\NormalTok{  ) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{expand\_limits}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{y =} \DecValTok{0}\NormalTok{) }\SpecialCharTok{+}
  \FunctionTok{theme\_minimal}\NormalTok{(}\AttributeTok{base\_size =} \DecValTok{12}\NormalTok{)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{figure}[H]

{\centering \pandocbounded{\includegraphics[keepaspectratio]{weeks/week10-reporting-publishing/report-template_files/figure-pdf/unnamed-chunk-4-1.pdf}}

}

\caption{Mean BMI by income group among adults age 20-80 in the prepared
NHANES Health Equity classroom dataset. Values are unweighted and

\end{figure}%

\section*{Limitations}\label{limitations}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Limitations}

\markright{Limitations}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The table and figure use a prepared classroom dataset rather than raw
  NHANES files.
\item
  The summaries are unweighted.
\item
  The comparison is descriptive and does not establish causality.
\item
  Missing BMI or income group values are excluded from the grouped
  summary.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Plain-Language Summary}\label{plain-language-summary}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Plain-Language Summary}

\markright{Plain-Language Summary}

In this classroom dataset, mean BMI differs across income groups. The
result is useful for practicing reproducible reporting and dashboard
interpretation, but it should be described as a descriptive pattern
rather than a causal finding.

\section*{AI-Use and Audit Note}\label{ai-use-and-audit-note}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{AI-Use and Audit Note}

\markright{AI-Use and Audit Note}

Document any AI assistance used to draft code, captions, or
interpretation. State exactly which output you checked against the code,
data dictionary, or rendered report.

\section*{Publishing Checklist}\label{publishing-checklist}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Publishing Checklist}

\markright{Publishing Checklist}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Report renders from source.
\item
  Citations resolve.
\item
  Figures and tables are generated by code.
\item
  Public preview contains no restricted data or private links.
\item
  Private preview access is granted to course staff when needed.
\item
  Canvas submission includes the repository link, report path, and
  latest commit hash.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Grounding Audit Prompt}\label{grounding-audit-prompt}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Grounding Audit Prompt}

\markright{Grounding Audit Prompt}

Select three claims from the report. For each claim, write the
supporting evidence source: code chunk, table, figure, or citation.
Revise any claim that cannot be traced to evidence in the report.

\part{Week 11: Portfolio Surgery}

\chapter*{Advanced Topics}\label{advanced-topics}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Advanced Topics}

\markboth{Advanced Topics}{Advanced Topics}

\section*{Overview}\label{overview-10}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Overview}

\markright{Overview}

Before the final submission, students must ``bulletproof'' their work.
This chapter focuses on advanced project hygiene, dependency management
(ensuring your code runs on someone else's machine), and auditability.
You will act as a peer reviewer to identify reproducibility failures in
others' code, fixing broken paths and missing seeds, to ensure your
final project stands up to scientific scrutiny.

\section*{Connection}\label{connection-10}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Connection}

\markright{Connection}

Week 10 produced a report with code-generated outputs and citations.
This week tests whether the report, dashboard, and instructions survive
a fresh environment. The final portfolio should leave a reviewer able to
rerun the core work without private setup knowledge.

\item
  Optionally draft a static dashboard extension using a CSV snapshot and
  no Shiny server.
\item
  Log any dependency, path, or README issues as reproducibility risks.
\item
  Use \href{reproducibility-test.qmd}{Reproducibility Stress Test} as
  the runbook.
\item
  Check final submission expectations in
  \href{../../milestones/final-portfolio/README.md}{Final Portfolio}.
\end{itemize}

Student output: a reproducibility test log, repaired README or path
issues, and a final risk list. Assignment link:
\href{../../assignments/assignment10-portfolio-surgery/README.md}{Assignment
10}.

\section*{Static Dashboard Demo}\label{static-dashboard-demo}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Static Dashboard Demo}

\markright{Static Dashboard Demo}

The completed \href{static-dashboard-ojs.qmd}{static dashboard source
file} demonstrates a no-server alternative that reads the NHANES CSV
snapshot in the browser. Use it as an exemplar for optional portfolio
extensions after the core Shiny and report workflows are reproducible.

\section*{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-7}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Definition of Done}

\markright{Definition of Done}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  A peer can clone or open the repository in a fresh Codespace and
  reproduce the core outputs.
\item
  Broken paths, missing dependencies, unclear READMEs, and ungrounded AI
  claims are fixed.
\item
  The
  \href{../../milestones/final-portfolio/final-portfolio-checklist.md}{Final
  Portfolio Checklist} is complete.
\end{itemize}

\chapter*{Reproducibility Stress
Test}\label{reproducibility-stress-test}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Reproducibility Stress Test}

\markboth{Reproducibility Stress Test}{Reproducibility Stress Test}

\section*{Goal}\label{goal-11}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Goal}

\markright{Goal}

Test whether a project can run in a fresh environment and document every
failure before final submission.

\section*{Runbook}\label{runbook}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Runbook}

\markright{Runbook}

Complete these steps in a new Codespace or equivalent clean environment.
Work from the repository root unless the project README says otherwise.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Open the repository from GitHub.
\item
  Read the README before running code.
\item
  Record the latest commit hash.
\item
  Install or restore dependencies using only documented instructions.
\item
  Render the final report.
\item
  Open or run the dashboard-style KT product.
\item
  Check that data paths are relative.
\item
  Check that citations resolve.
\item
  Check that figures and tables are generated from code.
\item
  Record every failure, warning, unclear instruction, and repair.
\item
  Commit repairs with clear messages.
\item
  Submit the updated repository link and commit hash on Canvas.
\end{enumerate}

\section*{NHANES Exemplar Checks}\label{nhanes-exemplar-checks}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{NHANES Exemplar Checks}

\markright{NHANES Exemplar Checks}

For the NHANES case study, use these checks:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  load \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  launch the Shiny exemplar from the repository root
\item
  open the static dashboard source in
  \texttt{weeks/week11-portfolio-surgery/static-dashboard-ojs.qmd}
\item
  render the Week 10 report template
\item
  confirm the README explains cached data and optional CDC retrieval
\end{itemize}

\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Sample Failure Log}

\section*{Peer Review Roles}\label{peer-review-roles}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Peer Review Roles}

\markright{Peer Review Roles}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Driver:} Runs the project exactly as documented.
\item
  \textbf{Navigator:} Reads instructions aloud and logs ambiguity.
\item
  \textbf{Auditor:} Checks paths, data provenance, citations, and AI-use
  notes.
\end{itemize}

Rotate roles after the first major failure or after 20 minutes.

\section*{Optional Python Pathway}\label{optional-python-pathway}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Optional Python Pathway}

\markright{Optional Python Pathway}

If the project uses Python, add a short dependency note with:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  how Python is launched
\item
  required packages
\item
  whether a notebook, script, or Quarto Python chunk is the primary
  workflow
\item
  how outputs are regenerated
\end{itemize}

Keep the Python pathway documented, but do not add it to a project
unless it supports the core audience-facing product.

\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Final Portfolio Readiness}

\markright{Final Portfolio Readiness}

Before final submission, complete the
\href{../../milestones/final-portfolio/final-portfolio-checklist.md}{Final
Portfolio Checklist} and review
\href{../../milestones/final-portfolio/repository-sharing-guidance.md}{Repository
Sharing Guidance}.

\section*{Final Repair Checklist}\label{final-repair-checklist}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Final Repair Checklist}

\markright{Final Repair Checklist}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item[$\square$]
  All required outputs render or run from a fresh environment.
\item[$\square$]
  README instructions match the actual workflow.
\item[$\square$]
  Data paths are relative.
\item[$\square$]
  Required cached data files or data access instructions are present.
\item[$\square$]
  Citations resolve.
\item[$\square$]
  AI-use notes identify what was drafted and how it was checked.
\item[$\square$]
  Remaining risks are documented in the failure log.
\end{itemize}

knowledge translation, reproducibility, and auditability.

should arrive with rendered slides and the project repository link ready
to share.

\markright{Presentation Rubric}

Use this rubric while preparing and while giving peer feedback:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  audience and KT purpose are clear
\item
  question is focused
\item
  data source and limitations are transparent
\item
  main visual is readable and evidence-based
\item
  interpretation is accurate and avoids unsupported causal language
\item
  privacy and stewardship risks are acknowledged
\item
  timing fits the presentation window
\item
  questions are answered with evidence and humility
\end{itemize}

Strongest evidence shown & \\
One interpretation or limitation to clarify & \\
One reproducibility or source question & \\
One audience-facing improvement & \\

\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Slide Checklist}

\markright{Slide Checklist}

Before presenting, check that:

\begin{itemize}

the syllabus.

\section*{Grading Logic}\label{grading-logic}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Grading Logic}

\markright{Grading Logic}

Assignments 1-3 must be completed to pass the course. They are not
numerically weighted, but they establish the workflow students need for
every later assignment.

Assignments 4-10 are scored on Canvas. Only the best 5 of these 7 scores
count toward the final grade, so students have flexibility while still
practicing consistently after the foundation weeks.

\section*{Common Reproducibility
Contract}\label{common-reproducibility-contract}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Common Reproducibility Contract}

\markright{Common Reproducibility Contract}

Every assignment must:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  run or render in GitHub Codespaces;
\item
  use relative paths rather than machine-specific paths;
\item
  keep source files and rendered outputs in the requested submission
  folder;
\item
  commit and sync the final work before submitting the repository link
  on Canvas;
\item
  document packages or dependencies when adding anything beyond the
  course defaults.
\end{itemize}

\section*{Common AI-Use Expectation}\label{common-ai-use-expectation}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Common AI-Use Expectation}

\markright{Common AI-Use Expectation}

AI may help students brainstorm, debug, translate, refactor, or improve
wording. Students remain responsible for checking every code path,
column name, statistic, citation, and interpretation. When AI is used,
the submission must include an AI-use note explaining what the tool
helped with, what changed, and how the result was verified.

\chapter*{Assignment Template}\label{assignment-template}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Assignment Template}

\markboth{Assignment Template}{Assignment Template}

Use this structure for weekly applied assignments. Keep the
student-facing brief short enough to act on, but specific enough that a
beginner can submit the right files without private instructions.

\section*{Title}\label{title}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Title}

\markright{Title}

Use:

\begin{Shaded}

\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Purpose}

\markright{Purpose}

Explain why the assignment matters, how it connects to the week, and how
it prepares students for the next week or a project milestone.

\section*{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-3}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Learning Objectives}

\markright{Learning Objectives}

Use 3-5 concise bullets.

\section*{Inputs}\label{inputs-2}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Inputs}

\markright{Inputs}

List week pages, data files, starter files, examples, templates, or
milestone pages. Use relative paths only.

\section*{Tasks}\label{tasks}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Tasks}

\markright{Tasks}

Provide a numbered workflow a beginner can follow.

\section*{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{AI-Use Expectations}

\markright{AI-Use Expectations}

State what AI may help with and what students must verify. Require an
AI-use or audit note when code, interpretation, debugging, writing, or
translation used AI support.

\section*{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Reproducibility Requirements}

\markright{Reproducibility Requirements}

State that the work must run or render in GitHub Codespaces, use
relative paths, and declare dependencies when new packages are added.

\section*{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{What To Submit}

\markright{What To Submit}

Give exact filenames and the exact folder:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignmentXX{-}short{-}name/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section*{Submission Route}\label{submission-route}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Submission Route}

\markright{Submission Route}

Students submit their GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing
and syncing the required files.

\section*{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Grading Checklist}

\markright{Grading Checklist}

For Assignments 1-3, use Complete/Incomplete. For Assignments 4-10, use
a practical 7-point checklist or a Complete/Needs Work checklist with

\chapter{Assignment 1: Modern
Workflows}\label{assignment-1-modern-workflows}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-4}

This assignment checks that your fork-based course workflow is working
before the analysis weeks begin. You will fork the course template
repository, launch or reopen a GitHub Codespace from your fork, edit a
Quarto document, render output, commit and sync your work, and stop the
Codespace when you are finished.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Fork the course template repository under your GitHub account.
\item
  Launch or reopen the correct Codespace from your fork.
\item
  Edit a Quarto document using YAML, Markdown, and a simple code chunk.
\item
  Render a Quarto document and locate the output file.
\item
  Commit and sync a small reproducible change.
\item
  Stop the Codespace to conserve free monthly hours.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 2 page: \texttt{weeks/week02-modern-workflows/index.qmd}
\item
  Course data example:
  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Submission folder:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment01-workflows/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Open the course template repository linked from Canvas.
\item
  Click \textbf{Fork} and create a personal fork under your GitHub
  account.
\item
  Launch Codespaces from your forked repository.
\item
  Create the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment01-workflows/submission/}.
\item
  Create a Quarto file named \texttt{workflow-check.qmd} in that folder.
\item
  Add a YAML title, your name, and the date.
\item
  Add one short paragraph explaining why relative paths matter in this
  course.
\item
  Add one code chunk that prints the current working directory or lists
  the files in the submission folder.
\item
  Render \texttt{workflow-check.qmd} to HTML.
\item
  Use the Source Control panel to inspect the diff.
\item
  Commit with a clear message such as
  \texttt{Complete\ Assignment\ 1\ workflow\ check}.
\item
  Sync/push the commit to your fork on GitHub.
\item
  Stop the Codespace after confirming the commit appears on GitHub.
\item
  Write a short reflection naming one workflow step that felt solid and
  one step you want to practice.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-1}

AI may help you understand an error message or explain Quarto syntax.
You must personally verify that the file renders and that the commit is
visible on GitHub. If AI helped, include one sentence in
\texttt{reflection.md} saying what it helped with and how you checked
the result.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Quarto file must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Paths must be relative to the repository.
\item
  The rendered HTML must be generated from the submitted \texttt{.qmd}
  file.
\item
  The final files must be committed and synced to your fork.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-1}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]

\chapter{Assignment 2: R With AI}\label{assignment-2-r-with-ai}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-5}

This assignment turns the Week 3 R basics into a small auditable
analysis note. You will load data with a relative path, inspect it
before summarizing, use \texttt{dplyr} verbs, write one custom function,
and document how any AI-generated code was checked.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Load R packages and import a CSV with a relative path.
\item
  Inspect rows, columns, variable types, and missingness before
  analysis.
\item
  Use \texttt{filter()}, \texttt{mutate()}, \texttt{group\_by()}, and
  \texttt{summarise()} for a descriptive summary.
\item
  Write one small custom function and test it on the dataset.
\item
  Audit AI-generated code for wrong column names, missingness mistakes,
  and overclaiming.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 3 page: \texttt{weeks/week03-r-with-ai/index.qmd}
\item
  Starter note:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment02-r-with-ai/starter-analysis.qmd}
\item
  Dataset: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Submission folder:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment02-r-with-ai/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-2}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment02-r-with-ai/submission/}.
\item
  Copy the starter structure into \texttt{analysis-note.qmd}.
\item
  Load \texttt{readr}, \texttt{dplyr}, and any other package used in the
  file.
\item
  Import the NHANES CSV using a relative path from the repository root.
\item
  Inspect the dataset with row count, column names, variable types, and
  missingness.
\item
  Create one cleaned or analysis-ready table using at least three
  \texttt{dplyr} verbs.
\item
  Write one custom function, such as a function that returns mean and SD
  for a numeric variable.
\item
  Save a small output file named \texttt{output/clean\_data.csv} or
  \texttt{output/summary\_table.csv}.
\item
  Add AI audit comments near code that was drafted, debugged, or revised
  with AI assistance.
\item
  Render the Quarto note to HTML.
\item
  Commit and sync the source, rendered output, saved output file, and
  AI-use note.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-2}

AI may help draft code, explain an error, or suggest a function
structure. You must verify that all column names exist, missing values
are handled intentionally, outputs match the code, and interpretations
remain descriptive. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md} even if AI was not
used.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-2}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The analysis must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  The data path must be relative.
\item
  Output files must be created by code, not manually edited.
\item
  New packages must be named in the Quarto file and in
  \texttt{dependency-note.md}.
\item
  \texttt{renv} awareness is introductory here: run
  \texttt{renv::status()} and note whether you used the existing course
  environment or added packages. Use \texttt{renv::snapshot()} only if
  you intentionally added a package.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-2}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment02{-}r{-}with{-}ai/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{analysis-note.qmd}: Quarto source with data import,
  inspection, cleaning, function, and AI audit comments.
\item
  \texttt{analysis-note.html}: rendered output.
\item
  \texttt{output/clean\_data.csv} or \texttt{output/summary\_table.csv}:
  code-generated output.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}: what AI helped with and how you checked it,
  or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\item
  \texttt{dependency-note.md}: packages used and whether any were added
  beyond the course environment.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-2}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-2}

Complete:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  CSV is imported with a relative path.
\item
  Dataset inspection includes size, column names/types, and missingness.
\item
  \texttt{dplyr} workflow uses at least three relevant verbs.
\item
  One custom function is defined and used.
\item
  Output CSV is generated by code.
\item
  AI-use note and dependency note are present.
\item
  Quarto file renders in Codespaces.
\end{itemize}

Incomplete:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Data path is absolute or machine-specific.
\item
  Code does not render.
\item
  Missingness is ignored or silently dropped.
\item
  Function is absent or unused.
\item
  Output file is missing or manually created.
\item
  AI-use or dependency note is missing.
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-12}

You are done when \texttt{analysis-note.qmd} renders, the output CSV is
regenerated by code, and the Canvas submission links to the synced
repository folder.

\chapter{Assignment 3: Git
Collaboration}\label{assignment-3-git-collaboration}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-6}

This assignment checks that you can make a small, reviewable change
using Git. You will create a branch, make a documentation-only edit,
inspect the diff, open a pull request, and connect the workflow to M0
group repository readiness.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Make meaningful commits with clear messages.
\item
  Read a diff before committing.
\item
  Use \texttt{.gitignore} to keep generated or sensitive files out of
  version control.
\item
  Create a branch and open a pull request.
\item
  Document a limited change so collaborators can review it.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 4 page: \texttt{weeks/week04-git-collaboration/index.qmd}
\item
  M0 connection: \texttt{milestones/m0-group-formation/README.md}
\item
  Submission folder: \texttt{assignments/assignment03-git/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-3}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create the folder \texttt{assignments/assignment03-git/submission/}.
\item
  Create a new branch with a short name such as
  \texttt{docs/git-audit-note}.
\item
  Make one documentation-only edit in your repository. Good choices
  include clarifying a README sentence, adding a path note, or improving
  a caption.
\item
  Inspect the diff and confirm the change is limited to documentation or
  text.
\item
  Check \texttt{.gitignore} and add a safe pattern if a generated or
  temporary file is being tracked.
\item
  Commit with a message that explains the change.
\item
  Push the branch and open a pull request.
\item
  In the pull request description, explain what changed and how you
  verified the scope.
\item
  Add a short note on whether your group repository is ready for M0:
  members, access, communication plan, and Codespaces access.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-3}

AI may help summarize a diff or draft a pull request description. You
must verify the diff yourself and make sure the pull request does not
include unrelated file changes. Include an AI-use sentence in
\texttt{git-audit-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-3}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  All changes must be visible in Git history.
\item
  The pull request must show a focused diff.
\item
  \texttt{.gitignore} must not hide source files needed to reproduce the
  work.
\item
  Evidence files must use relative paths or GitHub URLs.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-3}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment03{-}git/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{pull-request-evidence.md}: pull request URL, branch name, and
  final commit hash. If a pull request cannot be opened, include
  screenshot filenames and a written explanation.
\item
  \texttt{documentation-change.md}: the before/after text or a link to
  the changed documentation file.
\item
  \texttt{git-audit-note.md}: diff summary, \texttt{.gitignore} check,
  AI-use sentence, and M0 readiness note.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-3}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-3}

Complete:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Branch was created for the work.
\item
  Documentation-only change is focused and reviewable.
\item
  Diff was inspected and summarized.
\item
  Commit message is clear.
\item
  Pull request evidence or equivalent text evidence is included.
\item
  \texttt{.gitignore} was checked.
\item
  M0 readiness note names repository access and team communication
  status.
\end{itemize}

Incomplete:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Work was committed directly without evidence of a branch or review
  step.
\item
  Diff includes unrelated changes.
\item
  Pull request evidence is missing.
\item
  \texttt{.gitignore} check is absent.
\item
  M0 readiness note is missing.
\item
  Work is not synced to GitHub.
\end{itemize}

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-13}

You are done when the Canvas submission links to a synced repository
with pull request evidence, a documentation change summary, and a Git
audit note in the Assignment 3 submission folder.

\chapter{Assignment 4: Polyglot
Parity}\label{assignment-4-polyglot-parity}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-7}

This assignment strengthens R first, then translates a small descriptive
task to Python/pandas and checks whether both languages produce the same
answer. The goal is careful bilingual reading, not Python mastery.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-7}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Build a trusted R \texttt{dplyr} summary before translating it.
\item
  Refactor repeated R logic into a small function.
\item
  Translate a simple summary to Python/pandas with AI support if useful.
\item
  Run parity checks for row counts, group labels, and rounded
  statistics.
\item
  Keep notebook outputs and dependency notes clean for review.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 5 page: \texttt{weeks/week05-polyglot-r-deepening/index.qmd}
\item
  R deepening page:
  \texttt{weeks/week05-polyglot-r-deepening/r-deepening-parity.qmd}
\item
  Dataset: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Parity template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment04-polyglot/parity-table-template.csv}
\item
  Submission folder:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment04-polyglot/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-4}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment04-polyglot/submission/}.
\item
  Create \texttt{polyglot-parity.qmd}.
\item
  In R, load the NHANES CSV with a relative path.
\item
  Produce a descriptive BMI summary by \texttt{IncomeGroup} and
  \texttt{Gender}.
\item
  Refactor the R summary into one small reusable function.
\item
  Create \texttt{translation.ipynb} and translate the same summary to
  Python/pandas.
\item
  Create \texttt{helpers.py} with at least one reusable Python helper
  called from the notebook.
\item
  Complete a parity table comparing row counts, filtered row counts,
  grouping labels, and rounded summary values.
\item
  Write a dependency note naming Python packages used. Include
  \texttt{requirements.txt} only if you add packages beyond the course
  defaults.
\item
  Clear bulky notebook outputs before committing.
\item
  Render the Quarto file to HTML.
\item
  Commit and sync the assignment folder.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-4}

AI may help translate R to Python, explain tracebacks, or suggest helper
functions. You must verify that the translated code answers the same
question, uses the same filters, handles missingness the same way, and
returns matching grouped results after rounding. Include
\texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  R and Python files must run in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Paths must be relative to the repository.
\item
  The notebook must not contain large stale outputs.
\item
  Dependencies must be documented in \texttt{dependency-note.md}.
\item
  Differences between R and Python results must be explained before
  changing code.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-4}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment04{-}polyglot/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{polyglot-parity.qmd}: R analysis, R function, parity
  discussion, and rendered-output instructions.
\item
  \texttt{polyglot-parity.html}: rendered Quarto output.
\item
  \texttt{translation.ipynb}: Python/pandas translation notebook.
\item
  \texttt{helpers.py}: reusable Python helper script called from the
  notebook.
\item
  \texttt{parity-table.csv}: completed parity table.
\item
  \texttt{dependency-note.md}: Python/R dependency note, plus
  \texttt{requirements.txt} if extra Python packages were added.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}: what AI helped translate or debug and how you
  verified the result.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-4}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-4}

Canvas applies a 7-point rubric. This assignment is part of the
best-5-of-7 set for Assignments 4-10:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}lr@{}}

Criterion & Points \\
\midrule\noalign{}

\chapter{Assignment 5: Visualization
Audit}\label{assignment-5-visualization-audit}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-8}

This assignment turns a descriptive summary into an honest visual
explanation. You will create a \texttt{ggplot2} visualization, audit a
flawed plot, revise your own design, and write a caption that states the
statistic, data source, exclusions, and limitations.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Create a clear \texttt{ggplot2} visualization from the NHANES
  classroom dataset.
\item
  Identify misleading scale, title, grouping, missingness, and caption
  choices.
\item
  Revise a plot so the visual design matches the evidence.
\item
  Explain the descriptive and unweighted limits of the figure.
\item
  Connect visualization readiness to the M1 project proposal.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-7}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Dataset: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Worked example:
  \texttt{weeks/week06-visualization/viz03-worked-example.qmd}
\item
  Studio activity:
  \texttt{weeks/week06-visualization/viz06-studio-visual-audit.qmd}
\item
  Walkthrough and reference:
  \texttt{weeks/week06-visualization/viz07-assignment-a5-and-reference.qmd}
\item
  Flawed plot starter:
  \texttt{weeks/week06-visualization/instructor/flawed-plot-code.R}
\item
  Visual audit template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment05-visualization/visual-audit-note-template.md}
\item
  M1 connection: \texttt{milestones/m1-proposal/README.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-5}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment05-visualization/submission/}.
\item
  Review the Week 6 worked example.
\item
  Choose one descriptive comparison from the NHANES CSV snapshot.
\item
  Draft an initial plot using R and \texttt{ggplot2}.
\item
  Audit the flawed plot starter and your own draft for scale choices,
  labels, missing context, aggregation, and overclaiming.
\item
  Revise your plot so it supports a descriptive interpretation.
\item
  Export the corrected plot as \texttt{corrected-plot.png}.
\item
  Write a caption that includes data source, statistic, exclusions, and
  limitation.
\item
  Add an optional \texttt{plotly} preview only if it helps you inspect
  the figure; the required submission is still the static plot.
\item
  Write a short M1 link note naming how the visualization standard
  affects your project dataset, audience, or feasibility.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-5}

AI may help draft \texttt{ggplot2} code, suggest labels, or identify
possible visual problems. You must verify the code against real column
names, check that the scale does not exaggerate the result, and remove
causal or population-level claims that the plot cannot support. Include
\texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The Quarto file must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  The dataset path must be relative.
\item
  The plot image must be exported by code or a documented render step.
\item
  Captions must state that the analysis is descriptive and unweighted
  when using the classroom NHANES snapshot.
\end{itemize}

\section{What To Submit}\label{what-to-submit-5}

Place all submission files in:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[numbers=left,,]
\NormalTok{assignments/assignment05{-}visualization/submission/}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Submit these files:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{visualization-audit.qmd}: rerunnable Quarto file that loads
  the CSV, creates the final plot, and explains the revision.
\item
  \texttt{visualization-audit.html}: rendered output.
\item
  \texttt{corrected-plot.png}: exported final plot.
\item
  \texttt{visual-audit-note.md}: at least three issues checked or
  corrected.
\item
  \texttt{caption.md}: final figure caption.
\item
  \texttt{m1-link-note.md}: two or three sentences connecting the
  visualization standard to M1.
\item
  \texttt{ai-use-note.md}: what AI helped with and how you checked it,
  or a sentence saying AI was not used.
\end{itemize}

\section{Submission Route}\label{submission-route-5}

Submit your GitHub repository link on Canvas after committing and
syncing the required files.

\section{Grading Checklist}\label{grading-checklist-5}

Canvas applies a 7-point rubric. This assignment is part of the
best-5-of-7 set for Assignments 4-10:

\chapter{Assignment 6: EDA and AI
Audit}\label{assignment-6-eda-and-ai-audit}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-9}

This assignment builds a transparent preliminary analysis. You will
define a cohort, check missingness, create a Table 1-style descriptive
summary, audit planted errors, and revise a provenance/stewardship note
for reuse in M2.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-9}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Define an analysis cohort before summarizing results.
\item
  Report missingness for key variables.
\item
  Create a Table 1-style summary with N, mean, and SD.
\item
  Detect planted correctness, reproducibility, interpretation, and
  stewardship errors.
\item
  Write descriptive methods text that avoids causal overclaiming.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-8}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Dataset: \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}
\item
  Worked example:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda03-worked-example.qmd}
\item
  Studio activity:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda06-studio-planted-errors.qmd}
\item
  Walkthrough and reference:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/eda07-assignment-a6-and-reference.qmd}
\item
  Planted-error starter:
  \texttt{weeks/week07-eda-ai-audit/instructor/planted-error-starter.qmd}
\item
  Audit template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment06-eda-ai-audit/audit-note-template.md}
\item
  M2 connection: \texttt{milestones/m2-preliminary-analysis/README.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-6}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment06-eda-ai-audit/submission/}.
\item
  Load the NHANES CSV snapshot with a relative path.
\item
  Define and document an analysis cohort using age 20-80.
\item
  Create a missingness table for key variables.
\item
  Produce a Table 1-style summary by \texttt{IncomeGroup}.
\item
  Include N, BMI mean (SD), and age mean (SD).
\item
  Write a short methods note stating that the analysis is descriptive,
  unweighted, and non-causal.
\item
  Audit the planted-error starter and list at least five issues.
\item
  Categorize each issue as correctness, reproducibility, interpretation,
  or stewardship.
\item
  Revise the provenance/stewardship statement so it names the data
  source, classroom-use context, and one privacy or stewardship caution.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-6}

AI may help draft code, explain an error, or improve the organization of
the audit note. You must verify cohort filters, missingness handling,
labels, summary statistics, and interpretation against the code and
output. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-6}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  The EDA note must render in GitHub Codespaces.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  \texttt{table1.csv} must be generated by code.
\item
  Missingness handling must be stated before interpreting summaries.
\item
  The methods note must avoid causal and population-level claims
  unsupported by the unweighted classroom example.
\end{itemize}

  missingness table, Table 1, and methods note.
\item
  \texttt{eda-note.html}: rendered output.
\item
  \texttt{table1.csv}: exported Table 1 or equivalent descriptive
  summary.
\item
  \texttt{audit-note.md}: at least five planted issues with category,
  why it matters, and corrected approach.
\item

  for reuse in M2.
\item

\end{itemize}

\chapter{Assignment 7: Dashboard KT
Prototype}\label{assignment-7-dashboard-kt-prototype}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-10}

This assignment introduces dashboard-style Knowledge Translation. You
will run or render a small dashboard product, identify the audience and
question, document one control or interaction, improve one
audience-facing element, test the product, and state a limitation.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-10}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Explain how a dashboard differs from a static report.
\item
  Identify an audience-specific KT question.
\item
  Document one control, filter, or dashboard-style interaction.
\item
  Connect one visualization to a plain-language interpretation.
\item
  Test a dashboard product in Codespaces and record the result.
\end{itemize}

\item

  \texttt{examples/nhanes-equity/data/nhanes\_equity\_v6.csv}

  \texttt{assignments/assignment07-dashboard/dashboard-adaptation-template.md}
\end{itemize}

  (\texttt{weeks/week08-dashboard-kt/dash03-starter-dashboard.qmd}). The
  Shiny exemplar is a demonstration and is not an approved A7 submission

\item

  team, or briefing group.

\item

\item
  Identify one label, caption, help text, interpretation note, or README
  sentence that could be clearer for that audience.
\item

\item

\chapter{Assignment 8: Scientific
Communication}\label{assignment-8-scientific-communication}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-11}

This assignment turns one result from your analysis or dashboard into a
short audience-specific communication product. You will create a small
slide deck or slide outline, write a plain-language summary, cite the
data source, and use peer feedback to improve the claim.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-11}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Translate one technical result into a clear audience-facing takeaway.
\item
  Build a short Quarto revealjs deck or structured slide outline.
\item
  Use one figure or table from prior work as evidence.
\item
  State a limitation and source note beside the claim.
\item
  Give and apply peer feedback on clarity, evidence, and KT value.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-10}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 9 page: \texttt{weeks/week09-science-communication/index.qmd}
\item
  Peer feedback page:
  \texttt{weeks/week09-science-communication/revealjs-peer-feedback.qmd}
\item
  Renderable Week 9 slide starter:
  \texttt{weeks/week09-science-communication/starter-slides.qmd}
\item
  Slide starter:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/slide-outline-template.qmd}
\item
  Peer feedback template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/peer-feedback-template.md}
\item
  Prior work from Assignment 5, 6, or 7
\item
  Submission folder:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/submission/}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-8}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment08-communication/submission/}.
\item
  Choose one figure, table, or dashboard finding from prior work.
\item
  Define the audience in one sentence.
\item
  Write one plain-language takeaway that a non-technical reader can
  understand.
\item
  Create either \texttt{slides.qmd} as a revealjs deck or
  \texttt{slide-outline.qmd} as a structured slide outline.
\item
  Include 3-5 slides or slide sections: context, evidence, takeaway,
  limitation, and next step.
\item
  Add one source note or citation for the dataset.
\item
  Exchange peer feedback using the template.
\item
  Revise at least one sentence or slide based on feedback.
\item
  Document any AI assistance used for simplification, slide wording, or
  feedback synthesis.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-8}

AI may help simplify jargon, suggest slide structure, or tighten
wording. You must verify that the simplified text still matches the
evidence, citation, and limitation. AI must not invent findings,
sources, or audience needs. Include \texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-8}

  output.
\item
  Source notes and citations must be visible.
\item
  Paths must be relative.
\item
  Peer feedback must be specific enough to act on.
\end{itemize}

\chapter{Assignment 10: Portfolio
Surgery}\label{assignment-10-portfolio-surgery}

\section{Purpose}\label{purpose-13}

This assignment stress-tests your project before the final portfolio.
You will run the work in a fresh Codespace, repair dependency or path
failures, improve the README, complete peer review, and document
remaining risks for M4 and final submission.

\section{Learning Objectives}\label{learning-objectives-13}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Test a report and dashboard product in a clean environment.
\item
  Diagnose broken paths, missing dependencies, unclear README steps, and
  stale outputs.
\item
  Document failures and repairs in a reproducible audit trail.
\item
  Give and receive peer feedback on clarity, auditability, and KT value.
\item
  Create a final risk list before portfolio submission.
\end{itemize}

\section{Inputs}\label{inputs-12}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Week 11 page: \texttt{weeks/week11-portfolio-surgery/index.qmd}
\item
  Reproducibility protocol:
  \texttt{weeks/week11-portfolio-surgery/reproducibility-test.qmd}
\item
  Static dashboard exemplar:
  \texttt{weeks/week11-portfolio-surgery/static-dashboard-ojs.qmd}
\item
  M4 connection: \texttt{milestones/m4-peer-review/README.md}
\item
  Failure log template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment10-portfolio-surgery/failure-log-template.md}
\item
  Peer review template:
  \texttt{assignments/assignment10-portfolio-surgery/peer-review-template.md}
\end{itemize}

\section{Tasks}\label{tasks-10}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Create or use the folder
  \texttt{assignments/assignment10-portfolio-surgery/submission/}.
\item
  Open your project repository in a fresh Codespace or equivalent clean
  environment.
\item
  Follow only the README and documented setup instructions.
\item
  Render the report or book.
\item
  Run or render the dashboard-style product.
\item
  Check relative paths, package loading, citations, data availability,
  output freshness, and AI-use notes.
\item
  Log each failure before repairing it.
\item
  Repair dependency, path, README, citation, or stale-output issues.
\item
  Commit repairs with clear messages.
\item
  Exchange peer review focused on reproducibility, auditability,
  clarity, and KT value.
\item
  Write a final risk list naming remaining issues or stating that no
  known risks remain.
\end{enumerate}

\section{AI-Use Expectations}\label{ai-use-expectations-10}

AI may help interpret error messages, suggest README wording, or
organize the failure log. You must verify every repair by rerunning the
affected output in the clean environment. Include
\texttt{ai-use-note.md}.

\section{Reproducibility
Requirements}\label{reproducibility-requirements-10}

  environment.
\item
  Repairs must be committed and synced.
\item
  README instructions must match the tested workflow.
\item
  Relative paths must work from the repository root.
\item
  Any remaining risk must be documented rather than hidden.
\end{itemize}

Repairs address paths, dependencies, README, citations, or outputs &
1.25 \\
Report and dashboard product are rerun or failure is clearly explained &
1 \\
Peer review is specific, actionable, and tied to evidence & 1 \\
Final risk list is honest and useful for final portfolio planning & 1 \\
AI-use note and synced repair evidence are complete & 0.5 \\
\end{longtable}

\section{Definition of Done}\label{definition-of-done-20}

You are done when a reviewer can open the submission folder, see what
failed, see what was repaired, see what still needs attention, and
follow the README to reproduce the core project outputs.

\chapter{Milestones}\label{milestones}

\chapter{Milestones}\label{milestones-1}

Milestones move the term project from team setup to final portfolio.
They are short checkpoints, not separate side projects. Each one should
make the next stage easier to complete and easier for another person to
review.

\section{Submission Contract}\label{submission-contract}

For every milestone:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  commit and sync the required files before submitting
\item
  submit the GitHub repository link on Canvas
\item
  include the latest commit hash in the Canvas comment box
\item
  use relative paths
\item
  document any package or data access requirements
\item
  disclose and audit AI use
\end{itemize}

\section{Milestone Map}\label{milestone-map}

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}

  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\linewidth - 6\tabcolsep) * \real{0.2500}}@{}}

M0 & Week 4 & Group formation and repository access &
\href{m0-group-formation/README.md}{M0: Group Formation} \\

\href{m1-proposal/README.md}{M1: Project Proposal} \\

\href{m2-preliminary-analysis/README.md}{M2: Preliminary Analysis} \\
